L1095: Change from baseline is expressed as adjusted least squares mean (SE)
L1096: Source: Clinical Overview
L1097: Treatment-naïve (de novo) subjects
L1098: In Study 051, 20/116 subjects, and in Study 045, 21/80 subjects were treatment naïve for botulinum toxin. The starting dose of 500 U Dysport was established in Study 020 in 75 treatment-naïve subjects. The efficacy of the 500 U dose was confirmed for naïve subjects in both pivotal studies, and was their assessment scores were comparable to those of the previously treated subjects, in terms of the change from baseline in TWSTRS total score at Weeks 4, 8, and 12, and the secondary and tertiary/exploratory scores. There appears to be no substantial differences in the maintenance of effectiveness between the two groups in the Study 045b extended use.
L1099: Antibody production
L1100: Four subjects developed neutralizing antibodies after treatment in the pivotal studies. Only one of these subjects was a responder prior to developing neutralizing antibodies. In the one subject who exhibited a response, this response was lost at the time neutralizing antibodies were detected. Of the eight subjects with positive neutralizing antibody status at baseline, two were classified as responders. Six subjects with neutralizing antibodies to BTX-A at baseline were formally classified as "nonresponders" but continued treatment with Dysport. By the end of the open label phase of Study 045b, three subjects with previous negative neutralizing antibody test results had positive test results, and an additional two subjects that did not have baseline antibody status data). Open-label studies suggest that benefit from botulinum toxin treatment may not be directly affected by antibody production.
L1101: Extended Use of Dysport
L1102: Efficacy of Dysport was maintained across the three retreatment periods in the open label phase and the first three cycles of the extended use phase in Study 045b. Later cycles had too few subjects to draw meaningful conclusions. For each retreatment period in the open label phase, mean TWSTRS total score was lower than the mean score at baseline, and was statistically significant compared to baseline (p &lt; 0.001).
L1103: In the open-label phase of Study 045b (the possible 3 additional treatments), 2/131 (2%) of subjects withdrew due to lack of efficacy after only one treatment. The reasons for withdrawal were not reported for the extended use phase. During the extended use study, (up to 12 cycles), the median time to retreatment was determined to be 14 to 15 weeks. The higher doses (up to 1000 U) were associated with prolonged, and with improved symptom assessment by subjects and investigators. Pain relief did not appear to be dose dependent. Previous studies have shown that the higher doses are associated with increased AEs.
L1104: With the flexible dosing in Study 045b, less than half the subjects at Retreatment 2 (56/121 subjects) required an increased dose to 750 U or above. At Retreatment 3, 41% of subjects (45/111) continued to receive dose in the range 500 U to &lt;750 U. A dose of 1000 U was required in 26% (29/111). No subjects received doses &gt;1000 U in the open-label phase. Ten subjects received a dose of less than 500 U. The 500 U dose was established in Study 20 with treatment-naïve subjects. It remains the recommended starting dose for Dysport, but the open-label studies and literature suggest that higher doses are generally used in extended treatment.
L1105: Comparison to existing products on the market
L1106: There are few head-to-head comparisons of the botulinum toxins in controlled studies (see Lit. Review, Section ). Based on the few available, the safety profile of Dysport is similar to both Botox and Myobloc with the same AEs at similar frequency. The duration of efficacy does appear to be a little shorter with Dysport in the comparison studies, but similar to the 12 week duration asserted by Ipsen. The other botulinum toxins have established their claims for duration of action primarily on open-label use so no valid comparisons can be made.
L1107: 6.1.5 Clinical Microbiology
L1108: Not applicable for this product.
L1109: 6.1.6 Efficacy Conclusions
L1110: The study design was within the regulations on adequate and well-controlled studies (21 CFR 314.126). The sponsor supplied two adequate pivotal studies. The trials were multi-centered, sufficient in size for statistical analyses, suitable inclusion/exclusion criteria, well-randomized between placebo and Dysport groups for all significant baseline variables, and the clinical assessments and chosen endpoints were appropriate. The
L1111: selection of the study dose (500 U Dysport) was well supported by previous clinical trials, literature and post-marketing experience overseas. The protocol for the two pivotal studies were extremely similar in their study designs and all other variables (duration, endpoints, inclusion/exclusion criteria, demographics,...).
L1112: The efficacy conclusions for the primary endpoints showed Dysport more effective than placebo for treatment of CD at Week 4 analyzing either ITT or PP populations.
L1113: Consistent treatment benefit of Dysport compared to placebo was shown at the Week 8 and Week 12 assessments affirming duration of benefits. Although there was the “early exit factor” after Week 4, the majority of the subjects in both pivotal studies continued to receive botulinum toxin injections on the open-label studies, and the TWSTRS scores, VAS and QoL ratings showed benefits.
L1114: There were far more withdrawals of subjects from the placebo group than the Dysport group in both studies. The placebo group discontinuations consistently listed “lack of response as the reason for withdrawal from the controlled studies, and subsequent enrollment in the open-label studies. The Dysport group withdrawals were more frequently associated with non-drug issues (such as objection to blood draws, scheduling conflicts, etc..).
L1115: The secondary/exploratory endpoints assessments of change in TWSTRS subscales, symptoms, quality of life, and overall “treatment success” consistently favored treatment with Dysport rather than placebo whether done by subjects or investigators. Many of the secondary and exploratory endpoints reached levels of statistical significance.
L1116: There was no evidence of conflicting data in the efficacy studies.
L1117: The conclusions support the proposed indication. The findings of both pivotal studies are consistent with each other and with previous studies of Dysport and the other botulinum toxins. The secondary and tertiary/exploratory endpoint results support the primary endpoint results.
L1118: The limitations of available data are the weakness of Week 8 and Week 12 generalizations of the endpoints due to early exits from the studies. This limitation is more problematic for Study 045 than for Study 051.
L1119: Additional data is not necessary for the efficacy determinations. There are, however, questions that remain which involve:
L1120: • stability of the product which might affect potency over time. There have been inconsistencies in maintained stability in the trials for both Dysport and Reloxin (cosmetic) although the company eliminated the questionable batches from the statistical analyses of primary endpoints and separate analysis of the questionable batch in the Study 45 did not result in any significant differences in safety or efficacy. See CMC report.
L1121: • duration of effect which might be slightly shorter than with other botulinum products. However, the literature is inconsistent, and there are too few head-to-head studies to establish the average duration. The controlled studies submitted for Dysport are suggestive, but due to the study limitations can not be considered adequate to establish duration of action.
L1122: 7 INTEGRATED REVIEW OF SAFETY
L1123: 7.1 Methods and Findings
L1124: The safety review was conducted by Dr. Marc Stone, and will be filed separately.
L1125: 8 ADDITIONAL CLINICAL ISSUES
L1126: 8.1 Dosing Regimen and Administration
L1127: Drug-dose, drug concentration and relationship to response were not assessed in the pivotal studies. The Study 020 established the 500 U dose of Dysport as the recommended starting based on the responses of treatment-naïve subjects with CD tested at dosages of 250 U, 500 U and 1000 U. The open-label studies reinforce the feasibility of the 500 U dose as a starting dose. Nearly all the studies indicate that as the dosage increases, the effectiveness and the duration of action do increase, however the treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) also increase.
L1128: The UK label for Dysport carries the following recommendations for dosing:
L1129: “Adults and elderly: The doses recommended for torticollis are applicable to adults of all ages providing the adults are of normal weight with no evidence of low neck muscle mass. A reduced dose may be appropriate if the patient is markedly underweight or in the elderly, where reduced muscle mass may exist.
L1130: The initial recommended dose for the treatment of spasmodic torticollis is 500 units per patient given as a divided dose and administered to the two or three most active neck muscles.
L1131: For rotational torticollis distribute the 500 units by administering 350 units into the splenius capitis muscle, ipsilateral to the direction of the chin/head rotation and 150 units into the sternomastoid muscle, contralateral to the rotation.
L1132: For laterocollis, distribute the 500 units by administering 350 units into the ipsilateral splenius capitis muscle and 150 units into the ipsilateral sternomastoid muscle. In cases associated with shoulder elevation the ipsilateral trapezoid or levator scapulae muscles may also require treatment, according to visible hypertrophy of the muscle or electromyographic (EMG) findings. Where injections of three muscles are required, distribute the 500
L1133: units as follows, 300 units splenius capitis, 100 units sternomastoid and 100 units to the third muscle.
L1134: For retrocollis distribute the 500 units by administering 250 units into each of the splenius capitis muscles. This may be followed by bilateral trapezius injections (up to 250 units per muscle) after 6 weeks, if there is insufficient response. Bilateral splenii injections may increase the risk of neck muscle weakness.
L1135: All other forms of torticollis are highly dependent on specialist knowledge and EMG to identify and treat the most active muscles. EMG should be used diagnostically for all complex forms of torticollis, for reassessment after unsuccessful injections in non complex cases, and for guiding injections into deep muscles or in overweight patients with poorly palpable neck muscles. On subsequent administration, the doses may be adjusted according to the clinical response and side effects observed. Doses within the range of 250-1000 units are recommended, although the higher doses may be accompanied by an increase in side effects, particularly dysphagia. Doses above 1000 units are not recommended.
L1136: The relief of symptoms of torticollis may be expected within a week after the injection. Injections should be repeated approximately every 12 weeks or as required to prevent recurrence of symptoms.
L1137: 8.2 Drug-Drug Interactions
L1138: Drug-drug and drug-disease interactions were not assessed in the studies.
L1139: The concomitant use of anticholinergics, muscle relaxants, or aminoglycoside could potentially interact with Dysport.
L1140: 8.3 Special Populations
L1141: The pivotal studies reviewed for this application provided very little information on the use of Dysport for CD in ethnic minorities.
L1142: No studies of Dysport in pregnant or nursing women have been done. Dysport should be used in women who are pregnant, nursing, or who could become pregnant only if the potential benefit justifies the risk.
L1143: Dysport should be used only with extreme caution in patients with known or suspected neuromuscular disorders, with difficulty in swallowing or breathing due to the risk of aspiration.
L1144: Hypersensitivity reactions have been reported in post-marketing reports; although rare, some have been serious (but no reports of anaphylactic shock). Other rare serious adverse events reported in post-marketing include brachial plexopathy and 2 cases of neuralgic amyotrophy and Guillain-Barre Syndrome.
L1145: 8.4 Pediatrics
L1146: Dysport is currently marketed for use in the U.K. for the treatment of pediatric spasticity associated with cerebral palsy (for children ages \geq 3 years). The sponsor has not submitted data related to child or adolescent studies for review, or requested a pediatric indication.
L1147: A safety review is being conducted on the botulinum toxin products currently marketed in the US (Botox and Myobloc) and Dysport is included in the review. The review was triggered by reports of deaths in children following off-label injections of botulinum toxin for the treatment of spasticity.
L1148: 8.5 Advisory Committee Meeting
L1149: No Advisory Committee meetings are scheduled or anticipated for this product.
L1150: 8.6 Literature Review
L1151: A general literature review was done in the course of this review. In reporting, the focus is on the studies that did direct comparisons of Dysport to either Botox (botulinum toxin type A) or Myobloc (botulinum toxin type B), both of which are currently marketed in the US for treatment of CD. Summaries of the three most relevant studies are the following:
L1152: In a double-blind, randomized, parallel group study designed to evaluate whether a ratio of 3 units of Dysport is equivalent to 1 unit of Botox, patients with predominantly rotational cervical dystonia treated with either Botox (mean SD dose of 152 \pm 131 U) or Dysport (mean SD dose of 477 \pm 45 U) showed similar improvement, and no significant difference in safety profiles. With a peak effect at Week 4, subjects in both groups showed significant improvement in Tsui scores (Botox 28%, Dysport 29%). A global assessment of efficacy and safety considered 23/35 (66%) of the Botox and 29/38 (76%) of the Dysport subjects to be treatment successes (p=0.32). (5)
L1153: Comparative studies of botulinum neurotoxin (Botox, Dysport and Myobloc) in the treatment of cervical dystonia were reviewed in the published literature (70 articles) to compare rates of dysphagia and dry mouth. Botox was associated with a lower rate of dysphagia than Dysport (mean dysphagia rates of 8.9% for current Botox, and 26.8% for Dysport), both with p &lt; 0.05. Myobloc was associated more frequently with dry mouth, but it was not listed as a possible AE in many studies. The duration of effect seemed to be Botox &gt; Myobloc &gt; original Botox &gt; Dysport; all with p &lt; 0.001, but only 43 of the 70 studies reported duration. (2)
L1154: In a crossover comparison of Dysport and Botox on 54 patients with CD, Dysport was administered at a ratio of 1:3, and at 1:4 (1 U of Botox to 3 or 4 U of Dysport) and evaluated on the Tsui and TWSTRS pain scales. There was more improvement on impairment and pain with Dysport 1:3 (p=0.02 and 0.04 respectively) and 1:4 (p=0.01
L1155: and 0.02 respectively) then with Botox. The number of AEs was higher with both Dysport treatments. Dysphagia, the most frequently reported AE was 3%, 15.6%, and 17.3%, (Botox, Dysport 1:3 and 1:4, respectively). The study suggested that in cervical dystonia, the most appropriate conversion factor between Botox and Dysport units is less than 3. (6)
L1156: 8.7 Postmarketing Risk Management Plan
L1157: No post-marketing risk management plan has been proposed by the sponsor. Class labeling changes are currently under review at the FDA for all neurotoxin products to address the possibility of systemic spread of toxin. The safety data from Dysport was included in the class product review, and any changes affected in the future would include Dysport.
L1158: 8.8 Other Relevant Materials
L1159: The safety review done by Dr. Marc Stone notes the following:
L1160: “OSE recently completed a review of reports of iatrogenic botulism submitted by Solstice and Allergan, the sponsors of the two botulinum toxin products currently licensed in the U.S. The report concluded that there is a risk for the development of systemic botulism in patients receiving botulinum toxin injections. This risk is probably greatest in children treated for spasticity associated with cerebral palsy but has also been observed in adults. The dosages used appear comparable to dosages used to treat cervical dystonia. This application also refers to a case of systemic botulism in a patient treated with Dysport as well as the observation of muscular weakness remote to the injection site in a few Dysport-treated subjects; the findings of the review of the other botulinum toxin products should be applicable to Dysport.”
L1161: The CMC review by Dr. Susan Kirscher is not currently available, but should be checked for their assessment of product stability and unit standardization which has been under discussion during the review process.
L1162: 9 OVERALL ASSESSMENT
L1163: 9.1 Conclusions
L1164: • The two pivotal efficacy studies were adequately designed, conducted, and analyzed to support the conclusions based on the primary endpoint. The primary endpoint, reduction in the TWSTRS Total score at Week 4 for Dysport compared to placebo, was clinically and statistically significant in Study 051 (p &lt; 0.0001) and strongly suggestive for Study 045 (p = 0.013). The studies showed Dysport to be more effective than placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia.• The secondary and tertiary/exploratory endpoints were supportive of the efficacy finding, and there was no significant contradictory or inconsistent evidence that
L1165: emerged during any of the studies. The majority of the other endpoints reached statistical significance, and those that did not were similar in direction, and of sufficient magnitude to support the primary findings of efficacy.
L1166: • Although there were limitations in the data due to the possible early exit from studies after the Week 4 assessment, the duration of effectiveness in the pivotal studies and the open-label studies indicate that the recommended time to retreatment of ~ 12 weeks to 16 weeks (not &lt;12 weeks) as required is appropriate. There was maintenance of benefits at Week 8 and 12 assessments in both TWSTRS scores and VAS assessments.• Both efficacy and safety findings were comparable for treatment naïve and previously treated subjects.• There were no significant sub-group differences (based on demographics, baseline pain) that emerged in the studies.• The findings of the pivotal studies and open-label studies were consistent with the information found in studies with the other botulinum toxins.• Development of antibodies (seroconversion) was rare and does not correspond to adverse safety profiles.• Treatment associated adverse events were more common in the Dysport group. However, there were no reported SAEs (serious adverse events) associated with Dysport treatment. The adverse events (AE) profile of Dysport is similar to the other botulinum toxins (dysphagia, cervical muscular weakness, dry mouth, dysphonia, blurred vision, ...) in frequency and severity. Dysphagia occurred at rates similar to those found in the studies of other botulinum toxins. The majority of the AEs were mild or moderate in severity, and withdrawals from the studies due to adverse events were rare.• In the open-label studies, retreatment continued to show similar results to the initial study treatments. The open-label extension studies also indicate that flexibility of dosage, and time to retreatment is appropriate rather than a fixed dose and time-interval. The submitted studies and literature suggest that a starting dose of 500U Dysport could be recommended with subsequent adjusts of \leq 250U per session (max = 1000U).• For the treatment of cervical dystonia in adults, the benefit to risk ratio shown in the controlled studies is adequate for approval.• The safety review concluded that “There are no safety issues that would preclude the approval of Dysport for the treatment of cervical dystonia.”
L1167: 9.2 Recommendation on Regulatory Action
L1168: Based on the efficacy review, there are no issues that would preclude approval of the drug application of Dysport for the treatment of cervical dystonia in adults.
L1169: 9.3 Recommendation on Postmarketing Actions
L1170: 9.3.1 Risk Management Activity
L1171: No risk management activity is currently designated for this product.
L1172: 9.3.2 Required Phase 4 Commitments
L1173: The efficacy review has not resulted in any Phase 4 recommendations.
L1174: The safety review has recommended that there should be a commitment to five Phase 4 studies:
L1175: 1. A study of Dysport in patients with diabetes2. A study of changes in ventilation due to Dysport treatment including serum bicarbonate and arterial blood gas measurement and pulmonary function tests. If possible, the study should be enriched with subjects with mild obstructive lung disease.3. The effect of Dysport injection on vital signs in the immediate post-treatment period in patients with cervical dystonia4. A thorough QT study5. A study of safety and effectiveness of Dysport that includes substantial numbers of patients with African, Asian and Hispanic ancestry
L1176: 9.3.3 Other Phase 4 Requests
L1177: Recommendation has been made for further analysis of the effects of Dysport on liver-related biochemistry tests.
L1178: 9.4 Labeling Review
L1179: An annotated label indicating recommended changes to the sponsor's proposal is given in Appendix 10.4. Principal recommendations were based on the safety review, and include:
L1180: 1. Inclusion of class labeling discussing potential for systemic spread of toxin2. More detailed discussion of potential breathing and swallowing difficulties3. Inclusion of discussion of changes in blood glucose and electrocardiographic findings seen with Dysport treatment in the clinical development program.4. A request more a more extensive listing of adverse events reported in post-marketing experience.
L1181: 9.5 Comments to Applicant
L1182: Comments have been listed in Sections 9.3.2 and Appendix 10.4 regarding Phase 4 activities and labeling.
L1183: 10 APPENDICES
L1184: 10.1 Review of Individual Study Reports
L1185: 10.1.1 Study Y-47-52120-051 (Study 51):
L1186: A phase III multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study of the efficacy and safety of Dysport® for the treatment of cervical dystonia.
L1187: Study center(s) The study was conducted at sixteen centers in the USA (88 subjects) and four centers in Russia (28 subjects).
L1188: Sponsor Ipsen GroupBiomeasure Incorporated27 Maple StreetMilford, MA, 01757
L1189: Coordinating Investigator Daniel Truong, M.D.The Parkinson's and Movement Disorders Clinic9940 Talbert Av #204Fountain Valley, CA 92708
L1190: 10.1.1.1 Objective
L1191: To evaluate the efficacy and safety of intramuscular administration of Dysport (500 units) compared to placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia.
L1192: 10.1.1.2 Study Design
L1193: The study is similar in design to the previously conducted Phase III trial in the USA, Study Y-97-52120-045 (Study 45). The study was designed as a randomized, double-blind, multi-center (4 Russian and 16 US sites) trial of a single administration of Dysport (500 units) or placebo in adults with cervical dystonia. The study design used two parallel, balanced (~1:1 Dysport to placebo) groups.
L1194: 10.1.1.3 Study Population and Procedures
L1195: Study 51 screened 127 subjects and enrolled 116 subjects. They were randomized with 55 in the Dysport group and 61 in the placebo group. The subjects received a single dose of placebo or Dysport 500U (divided among two to four clinically indicated cervical muscles, with or without EMG guidance). Randomization was randomized by clinic site and whether the subject had previously received treatment with botulinum toxin.
L1196: Study visits were to include the screening visit within one week prior to the study treatment, the baseline visit (with treatment) at Week 0, and follow-up visits at Week 4, Week 8 and Week 12, or until retreatment if done before Week 12. In addition, a telephone contact for collection of adverse events (AEs) was planned for Week 2.
L1197: 10.1.1.3.1 Study duration
L1198: Date of first enrolment: October 10, 2005
L1199: Date of last completed: September 07, 2006
L1200: 10.1.1.3.2 Entry criteria
L1201: Inclusion Criteria
L1202: 1. Signed informed consent.2. Male or female of 18 years of age or older.3. Cervical dystonia with at least 18 months since onset, and previously untreated with botulinum toxin or previously treated with BTX-A or botulinum toxin type B (BTX-B) with a minimum interval of 16 weeks since the last injection and having returned at least to their usual pretreatment status.4. TWSTRS scores which met the following criteria at baseline:- TWSTRS total score \geq 30- TWSTRS severity subscale score \geq 15- TWSTRS disability subscale score \geq 3.
L1203: Exclusion Criteria
L1204: 1. Pure anterocollis or pure retrocollis.2. Apparent remission from cervical dystonia at time of screening.3. Previous poor response (determined by standard practice at each site, e.g., &lt;20\% improvement in TWSTRS total score from baseline to Week 4) to the previous two BTX-A or BTX-B treatments.4. Known requirement for fewer than 80 or more than 250 units of BOTOX® injected into the neck muscles, or fewer than 4000 or more than 12,500 units of BTX-B.5. Being treated with BTX-B due to lack of efficacy to BTX-A or having known neutralizing antibodies to BTX-A.6. Requirement for botulinum toxin injection to site(s) of the body other than the neck and unable to avoid such treatment(s) for the duration of the study.7. Known hypersensitivity to botulinum toxin or related compounds, or any component in the study drug formulation.8. Known significant underlying swallowing or respiratory abnormality which might be exacerbated by botulinum toxin treatment.9. Myasthenia gravis, other disease of the neuromuscular junction or
L1205: clinically significant, persistent neuromuscular weakness, or disease or symptoms that could interfere with the TWSTRS scoring.
10. Total body weight less than 100 lbs (45.4 kg).
11. Previous phenol injections to the neck muscles.
12. Previous myotomy or denervation surgery involving the neck or shoulder region.
13. Cervical contracture that limits passive range of motion.
14. Treatment with aminoglycoside antibiotics within the 30 days prior to study treatment.
15. Current or expected requirement for concomitant medication that may have interfered with the evaluation of study treatment (e.g., narcotics) (NOTE: Muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines were permitted if the dosage had been stable for 6 weeks prior to study treatment and was expected to remain at this stable dose until the Week 4 assessment. Every effort was to be made to keep concomitant cervical dystonia treatment constant throughout the study; however, changes in pain medication were acceptable if absolutely necessary according to clinical judgment).
16. Previously received any investigational new drug or device within 30 days prior to inclusion in the study.
17. Previously treated in this study.
18. Females who were pregnant or lactating. Women of child-bearing potential must have had a negative prestudy urine pregnancy test and subjects, or their partner, must have agreed to use adequate contraception (hormonal or barrier method of birth control) prior to injection of the study drug and for the duration of study participation. Non-child-bearing potential was defined as post-menopause for at least 1 year, surgical sterilization at least 3 months prior to screening, or hysterectomy.
19. Any medical condition or laboratory finding that compromised compliance with the objectives and procedures of this protocol or precluded the administration of botulinum toxin, as judged by the investigator.
20. In the opinion of the investigator, subjects who were unable and/or unwilling to comply fully with the study protocol (Section 16.1.1) and the study instructions.
L1206: 10.1.1.3.3 Study medications
L1207: • Dysport 500 U
• Placebo (125 micrograms of human albumin and 2.5 mg of lactose)
L1208: The contents of each vial were stored at 2 to 8°C and reconstituted with 1.0 mL Sodium Chloride Injection BP (0.9%), and treatment administered within 1 hour of reconstitution.
L1209: The sponsor states that the onset of action for Dysport is usually evident within 2 weeks, peak effect occurs at 4 to 8 weeks, and subjects generally require retreatment ~ every 12 weeks.
L1210: Test product, dose and mode of administration, batch number
L1211: A single dose of Dysport 500 units was to be administered by intramuscular injection divided between a maximum of four clinically indicated muscles. The dose of 500 U of Dysport for the treatment of cervical dystonia was established in previous studies, and is the recommended dosage currently in use in the other countries where Dysport is licensed.
L1212: Dysport batch IB05.001, manufactured at Ipsen Biopharm, was used for this study. Previous studies used an initial fixed dose of 500 units of Dysport manufactured at (b) (4). This study planned to evaluate the efficacy and safety of a fixed dose of 500 units of Dysport manufactured at IBL, Wrexham.
L1213: Dysport was supplied as a lyophilized white powder containing ~500 units (one unit is defined as the median lethal intraperitoneal dose in mice) of Clostridium botulinum toxin type A-hemagglutinin complex with 125 microg. of human albumin and 2.5 mg of lactose in an individual clear glass vial. The placebo supplies were presented in identical glass vials, containing 125 microg. of human albumin and 2.5 mg of lactose. The contents of each vial were reconstituted with 1.0 mL Sodium Chloride Injection BP (0.9%), and injection of subjects was to be done within 1 hour of reconstitution. Study drugs were to be stored at 2 to 8°C prior to use.
L1214: Previous studies have shown a decrease in treatment benefits over time with retreatment required approximately every 12-15 weeks. Blood concentration measurements were not planned for the study since Dysport is administered directly into the affected muscles, and is not expected to be present in peripheral blood at measurable levels.
L1215: Prohibited concurrent therapy
L1216: See inclusion/exclusion criteria. Concomitant use of aminoglycoside antibiotics or any other drug known to interfere with neuromuscular transmission was not allowed.
L1217: Permitted concomitant therapy
L1218: Muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines were permitted if the dosage was stable for 6 weeks prior to study treatment and remained stable until the Week 4 assessments. All other concomitant therapy was to be permitted, but should be kept at a constant dose; however, the investigator was able to alter concomitant medication at any time, based on clinical situation.
L1219: 10.1.1.3.4 Study procedures
L1220: Across the 20 centers, 127 subjects were screened, and 116 were randomized (the ITT population). Fifty-five (55) subjects were enrolled in the Dysport group, and 61 subjects in the placebo group. All 116 subjects received study medication and were included in the safety population, and 91 were included in the per protocol (PP) population.
L1221: Randomization was stratified by center site and whether the subjects were naïve or previously treated with botulinum toxin. After randomization to groups, an intramuscular injection of Dysport (500 units) or placebo was administered divided among 2 to 4 clinically indicated cervical muscles, with or without electromyogram (EMG) guidance.
L1222: Study visits were to include a screening visit, within 1 week prior to the study treatment, a baseline treatment visit at Week 0; follow up visits were planned for Weeks 4, 8, and 12 post-treatment or until retreatment (if done prior to Week 12). Subjects that completed the study without experiencing serious adverse events were eligible for inclusion in the open-label extension study.
L1223: Planned efficacy evaluations included subject and investigator visual analog scale (VAS) for symptom assessments, Toronto Western Spasmodic Torticollis Rating Scale (TWSTRS) total and subscale scores, VAS for pain, and SF-36 quality of life questionnaire (SF-36). Safety evaluations included clinical physical and neurological examinations, laboratory tests (including botulinum toxin antibody titers, and pregnancy testing for females) and collection of information on adverse events.
L1224: Schedule
L1225: The planned schedule of assessments is shown below in Table (9).
L1226: Included in the screening procedures were a review of cervical dystonia history, medical history, medication history, neurological and physical examinations (including vital signs), blood and urine collection for routine laboratory tests (including chemistry, hematology and urinalysis), and a urine pregnancy test for female subjects of child-bearing potential.
L1227: The baseline visit, prior to treatment, planned for a blood sample for BTX-A antibody testing, and the baseline evaluations of subject and investigator visual analog scale (VAS) for symptom assessments, Toronto Western Spasmodic Torticollis Rating Scale (TWSTRS) total and subscale scores, VAS for pain, and SF-36 quality of life questionnaire (SF-36).
L1228: At Week 2, a standardized telephone evaluation of adverse events (AEs) was planned.
L1229: The Week 4, 8, and 12 visits, were to include TWSTRS total and subscale scores, VAS for pain, and subject and investigator's VAS for symptom assessments. At Weeks 4 and 12, blood and urine were to be collected for routine laboratory tests (including chemistry, hematology, and urinalysis) and a blood sample was to be drawn for BTX-A antibody testing. The investigator's global assessment of pain and symptoms was to be performed at Week 12, and SF-36 at Week 8. Safety evaluations were to include neurological and physical examinations (including vital signs). AEs were to be collected throughout the study.
L1230: Subjects withdrawing from the study prematurely, or retreated prior to the Week 12 visit, were to be encouraged to undergo all procedures required for the Week 12 visit.

L1235: Source: Study Y-47-52120-051
L1236: Assessments
L1237: (1) TWSTRS
L1238: The degree of cervical dystonia was to be assessed by the use of the TWSTRS (total score, severity, disability, and pain subscales). The TWSTRS scale [Appendix 2] is more commonly used in studies of cervical dystonia in North America, and was selected for use in this study with the agreement of the FDA. The Tsui scale is generally the scale used in Europe, and was used by Ipsen in previous studies. The TWSTRS rating scale has 3 sections:
L1239: • Cervical Dystonia Severity Scale (maximum = 35) – with subsection evaluations of maximum excursion, duration factor, effect of sensory tricks, shoulder elevation/anterior displacement and range of motion
L1240: • Disability Scale (maximum = 30) – with subsection evaluations of work, activities of daily living, driving, reading, watching television, and activities outside the home
• Pain Scale (maximum = 20) – with subsection evaluations of severity of pain, duration of pain, and disability due to pain
(note: 0 = best score in all)
L1241: (2) Visual Analog Scale (VAS) for Pain
L1242: Subjects were to be asked to provide a retrospective assessment of pain (on average) on a VAS (0 mm to 100 mm) over the 7 days immediately prior to the study treatment at baseline, and at Weeks 4, 8, and 12 after study treatment. Subjects were to draw a single vertical slash through the line with a scale of 0 mm (no pain) to 100 mm (worst possible pain) that best represented the severity of their pain.
L1243: (3) Visual Analog Scale (VAS) for Symptom Assessment
L1244: Both subjects and investigators were to assess the severity of the cervical dystonia symptoms at baseline and at Weeks 4, 8, and 12 (or end of study visit). Rating was evaluated on the 0 – 100 mm scale described above.
L1245: The subject was to be asked: “Please assess the severity of your cervical dystonia over the last 4 days”. The subject was to complete the rating prior to and independently of the investigator.
L1246: The investigator was then to use a similar scale when asked to “Please assess the severity of the subject’s cervical dystonia today”.
L1247: (4) Investigator Global Assessment
L1248: The investigator global assessment is an end-of-study subjective measurement of the efficacy and safety of the treatment. Efficacy and safety are each rated on a five-point ordinal scale to assess the amount of change attributed to treatment (i.e., none, mild, moderate, severe, or extreme).
L1249: Treatments Administered
L1250: The complete treatment dose of Dysport 500 units, or placebo, was to be administered by intramuscular injection into the clinically indicated neck muscles in a single dosing session. The number of injection sites and the dose at each site were to be determined by the investigator during the baseline visit and recorded in the CRF.
L1251: Injection was to occur into two to four of the muscles specified below in accordance with the investigator’s normal practice, with or without EMG guidance:
L1252: • Right/Left Levator scapulae
• Right/Left Trapezius
• Right/Left Sternocleidomastoid
• Right/Left Splenius capitis
• Right/Left Scalenus (medius and anterior)
• Right/Left Semispinalis capitis
• Right/Left Longissimus capitis.
L1253: (Note: the left and right muscle of a muscle group were to be considered two muscles).
L1254: 10.1.1.3.5 Efficacy parameters
L1255: Primary Efficacy Variable
L1256: Change from baseline to Week 4 in TWSTRS total score.
L1257: Secondary Efficacy Variables
L1258: • Change from baseline in TWSTRS total score (for Weeks 2, 6, 8, and 12),• Subject and investigator visual analog scale (VAS) symptom assessment of cervical dystonia,• SF-36 quality of life (QoL) scores.• Proportion of subjects considered by the investigator to be overall treatment successes at the end of the study.
L1259: Exploratory Efficacy Variables
L1260: • The change from baseline in subject's VAS for pain and TWSTRS severity,• Disability and pain subscales scores,• Change in subject's VAS for pain for the subgroup of subjects with a VAS for pain greater than 40 mm at baseline,• Proportion of responders at each visit and time to retreatment.
L1261: 10.1.1.3.6 Statistical analysis
L1262: Statistical methods
L1263: The efficacy variables were to be assessed using the intent-to-treat (ITT) population, with a supportive analysis based on the per protocol (PP) population for the primary endpoint. Safety assessments were to be based on the safety population (all patients who received the study drug).
L1264: The primary and secondary efficacy variables were to be analyzed using an analysis of covariance, except for the assessment of treatment successes which was to use a logistics model.
L1265: Exploratory efficacy and safety variables were to be summarized by descriptive statistics, treatment differences with 95% confidence intervals, and shift tables.
L1266: No interim analysis was planned.
L1267: Determination of sample size
L1268: Sample size estimation was based on the primary efficacy variable, the change from baseline in the TWSTRS total score, for which placebo response estimate is 2.36 and Dysport response estimate is 8.76, with a common SD of \pm 9.38. These estimates are based on the data from a previous study with the same variable in a similar
L1269: population for subjects not naïve to botulinum toxin A.
L1270: The study was designed to detect a difference of 6.4 with 90% power using a two-sided test at the 5% significance level. Accordingly, 120 subjects were to be recruited, to allow for the completion of 47 subjects per treatment group.
L1271: 10.1.1.3.7 Protocol amendments
L1272: • May 31, 2006 - Amendment No. 1, administrative changes were noted and an update to the Subject Informed Consent form, providing further information on the potential for AEs due to study treatment.• January 11, 2007 - Amendment 2, RAP (reporting and analysis plan) amendment, details in next section
L1273: 10.1.1.3.8 Changes to the planned statistical analysis
L1274: After discussions with the FDA during the Dysport pre-biological license application (BLA) meeting, a change was made to the content of the secondary and exploratory endpoints planned in the protocol and the analysis planned in the final RAP. A number of the exploratory endpoints were escalated to secondary, with all secondary analyses adjusted for multiplicity.
L1275: The secondary endpoints were ranked in order of priority and each was tested at the 5% level. Each endpoint was tested in the order of the pre-determined hierarchy. All analyses were presented; however, no claims of significance were made from analyses ranked lower than the first non-significant result once that non-significant result had been encountered.
L1276: The RAP detailed that centers with fewer than six subjects were to be pooled based on geographical locations (i.e., within the same country). Ten centers randomized fewer than six subjects but these data were not pooled. It was decided not to re-analyze the data with the centers grouped as this could not be done before unblinding and was considered unlikely to have a significant effect on the conclusions.
L1277: 10.1.1.4 Study Results
L1278: 10.1.1.4.1 Trial characteristics
L1279: The trial was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group (~1:1 Dysport to placebo subjects) trial with adult cervical dystonia subjects.
L1280: 10.1.1.4.2 Demographics
L1281: Baseline Demographic Characteristics
L1282: Demographic characteristics for the ITT/safety population are summarized in Table 10. The majority of the subjects (75 of 116, 65%) were female, and all subjects were Caucasian in race, 7/116 (6%) were of Hispanic/Latino ethnicity. The mean age of the subjects was 53 \pm 12.9 years (~52 years for the Dysport group, and ~54 years for the placebo group). The Dysport and placebo groups were generally similar with respect to sex, race, ethnicity, height, and weight.

L1288: Baseline Medical Comparison
L1289: Baseline characteristics for the safety population are generally comparable for the two groups in the comparison of physical and neurological examinations, and medical and surgical history (Table 11).
L1290: A medical or surgical history was reported by 50 (91%) of the Dysport subjects and 52 (85%) of the placebo subjects. The two treatment groups were reasonably comparable in their medical histories. The system which had the greatest difference between groups was vascular disorders, with an incidence of 24/55 (44%) in the Dysport group and 15/61 (25%) in the placebo group. A history of depression and migraines was reported more frequently in the Dysport group.

L1295: As shown in Table 12, the Dysport and placebo groups were similar with respect to both cervical dystonia history, and previous botulinum toxin treatment for the safety population. The mean time since onset of signs/symptoms of cervical dystonia was almost 12 years for both groups, and the majority of the subjects (96/116, 83%) had previously been treated with botulinum toxin, (i.e., were "non-naïve"). The 20 subjects that had not previously received treatment with botulinum toxin (the treatment-naïve/de novo subjects) were evenly divided with 10 each in the Dysport and placebo groups.

L1299: Botox was the most common previous treatment, taken by 89/96 (93%) non-naïve subjects. Dysport had been received by 21/96 (22%) non-naïve subjects. The percentages of subjects previously taking Botox and Dysport were comparable across the Dysport and placebo groups; however, the percentage previously taking botulinum toxin type B was considerably higher in the placebo group with 12/51 of the non-naïve subjects (24%) compared to the Dysport group's 4/45 subjects (9%).
L1300: Prior and Concomitant Medication
L1301: Most of the study population (105/116, 91%) had taken some form of prior medication (i.e., within the 30 days prior to the baseline visit). The most frequently used prior medications were: anti-epileptics, taken by 43/116 (37%) subjects of the study population, vitamins (28/116, 24%), lipid modifying agents (27/116, 23%), analgesics (26/116, 22%), and anti-inflammatory and antirheumatic products (23/116, 20%).
L1302: The use of prior medications was generally comparable between the two treatment groups. Anti-epileptics were used by 23/55 (42%) subjects in the Dysport group compared to 20/61 (33%) subjects in the placebo group. The anti-epileptic medications were mostly prescribed for cervical dystonia. Vitamins and mineral supplements were more commonly used in the Dysport group, 17/55 (31%) vs. 11/61 (18%) in the placebo group.
L1303: Study Results
L1304: Subject Disposition
L1305: There were 10 withdrawals in the Dysport group (18%), and 23 withdrawals in the placebo group (23%) prior to the end of the study (Table 13). None of the withdrawals was attributed to adverse events. Withdrawals due to lack of treatment effectiveness accounted for 5 subjects (9%) of the Dysport population and 23 (38%) of the placebo population. The subjects that completed Week 4 assessments (essential to the calculation of the primary endpoint) represent 93% of each treatment group.

L1308: a Principal Investigator
L1309: Source: Study Y-47-52120-051
L1310: Of the ITT/Safety population, 14 Dysport subjects (25%), and 11 placebo subjects (18%) were excluded from the per protocol (PP) population for the primary endpoint. The reasons for the exclusions are shown in Table 14, and were most frequently due to determination of factors that violated the inclusion/exclusion criteria. The exclusions resulted in a PP population of 91 subjects (41 Dysport subjects and 50 placebo subjects). The sponsor calculated the primary study outcome (change in the TWSTRS Total Score from baseline) for both the ITT and the PP populations. Since there was not a statistically significant difference between the outcomes for the populations, the analysis of the PP populations was not extended to the secondary and exploratory endpoints.

L1315: 10.1.1.4.3 Protocol amendments and violations
L1316: Dosage levels
L1317: The following subject randomization errors occurred:
L1318: • Subject 015/00143 was randomized to the correct strata (not previously treated with botulinum toxin) but randomized out of sequence, and received #20059 instead of #20058.
• Subject 016/00151 was randomized to the correct strata (previously treated with botulinum) but randomized out of sequence, and received #10064 instead of #10061.
• Subject 019/00181 was randomized incorrectly and out of sequence. This subject had been previously treated with botulinum toxin, but was assigned to the not previously treated strata, and received #20074 instead of #10073.
L1319: The full prepared injection volume was not used in 3 Dysport subjects and 4 placebo subjects. The three Dysport subjects received 350, 425 and 450 units. The placebo subjects received 300, 400, 450, and 450 units of placebo.
L1320: Dr. Lew (Site 012) reported being unable to draw the full volume into the syringe. A total injection volume of less than 1 mL prepared volume was administered to 7 subjects from this site who may have received less than 500 units. The variance is documented in the file and in monitoring notes. One patient 009/00083 received 500 units but the reconstitution and injection volume were recorded as 2 mL.
L1321: 10.1.1.4.4 Efficacy endpoints
L1322: Evaluation of Efficacy
L1323: All efficacy analyses were conducted on the ITT population for the Dysport and placebo groups. Analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint was also performed on the PP population.
L1324: The analysis strategy was determined following discussions with the FDA (December, 2006). The efficacy endpoints were ranked by the primary endpoint, then secondary endpoints in the pre-determined order of priority, and tested sequentially for statistical significance at the 5% level. The results are presented in Figure 2 and Table 15.

L1336: Evaluation of the primary objective
L1337: Change from Baseline in TWSTRS Total Score at Week 4
L1338: The primary efficacy variable was the change from baseline of the TWSTRS total score at Week 4 on the ITT population. Analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) was used for assessment by treatment group, baseline TWSTRS score and by status of previous treatment by botulinum toxin. The analysis was also applied to subjects in the PP population for the primary efficacy variable. Since a significant difference was not detected between the ITT and PP populations on the primary efficacy endpoint, only the ITT population was used for the subsequent analyses of the secondary and exploratory variables.
L1339: For the subjects in who had withdrawn before the Week 4 assessments (4 in each group), the worst case score was imputed, and a sensitivity analysis performed for the primary efficacy variable.
L1340: The primary endpoint for the study, change from baseline in the TWSTRS Total Score, is statistically significant for the Dysport group compared to the placebo group (see Table 16). The TWSTRS total score change (baseline to Week 4) for the ITT population showed Dysport (n=51) had a mean change from baseline of -13.995, and the placebo group (n=58) had a mean change of -5.228 (p &lt; 0.0001). The p-value when analyzed by baseline score was p=0.015, by previous botulinum treatment p=0.405, and by center p=0.130.
L1341: The sponsor also applied the same evaluation to the PP population to evaluate whether the exclusions might have influenced the outcome. The Dysport PP group (n=41) had a mean change from baseline of -15.594, and the placebo group (n=50) had a mean change of -5.025 (p &lt; 0.0001). Since the outcome was virtually unchanged from the results of the ITT population, the analysis of the PP population was not applied to the secondary endpoints. For the PP population, the p-value when analyzed by baseline score was p=0.009, by previous botulinum treatment p=0.657, and by center p=0.169.
L1342: Evaluation of the secondary and exploratory endpoints
L1343: The secondary efficacy endpoints were tested on the ITT population only. Last observation carried forward (LOCF) values were used for missing values. The same statistical method (ANCOVA of change with group, week, baseline values, center, and previous treatment) was applied for analysis of all the secondary endpoints except endpoint 10. Endpoint 10, analysis of SF-36, involved derived calculations for the eight individual domains (derived from specific questions, and also grouped into mental and physical summary scores).
L1344: Change from Baseline in TWSTRS Total Score at Weeks 8 and 12
L1345: The mean\pmSD TWSTRS total score at baseline was 43.83 (\pm 7.97) in the Dysport group and 45.81 (\pm 8.78) in the placebo group. The results for change from baseline in TWSTRS total score at Week 4 (primary endpoint) and at Weeks 8 and 12 (secondary efficacy endpoints) are summarized in Table 16 for the ITT population. The TWSTRS
L1346: total score improved (decreased) more for the Dysport group than the placebo group with the change reaching statistical significance at Weeks 4 and 8 (p &lt; 0.0001 for each) and Week 12 (p = 0.019). At Week 8, the change in both the Dysport group and the placebo group were very similar to their Week 4 changes. The data suggest that treatment efficacy did not decline significantly by Week 8, but did show a decline by Week 12. However, the Week 12 endpoint still showed statistical significance for efficacy.

L1350: 11.4.1.2 Change from Baseline in Subject's VAS for Symptom Assessment
L1351: The subject's assessment of cervical dystonia symptoms severity at Weeks 4, 8 and 12, or end of study, were secondary efficacy endpoints and all showed statistical significance for the treatment group. The assessment was made using a VAS of 'no symptoms' (0 mm) to 'worst possible symptoms' (100 mm). The results from this are
L1352: presented in Table 17. The Dysport subjects reported an improvement in symptoms severity represented by about a fourth of the scale at Week 4 (-23.1 mm) and Week 8 (-24.6 mm), statistically significant at p &lt; 0.001. The improvement at Week 12 reported for the Dysport group (-16.4) compared to the placebo group (-5.4) had a p-value of 0.007.

L1365: Change from Baseline in SF-36 Scores
L1366: Change from baseline in SF-36 scores at Week 8 was a secondary efficacy endpoint.
L1367: The SF-36 QoL scores for mental and physical health are summarized in Table 19.
L1368: There are eight individual domains in the assessment: physical function, role function as limited by physical problems, bodily pain, general health perceptions, vitality, social functioning, mental health and role function as limited by emotional problems. These are condensed into the mental and physical health scores. The QoL scores are on a scale from 0 (worst health possible) to 100 (best health possible). The calculations of the individual domains were derived from the SF-36 Health Survey Manual and Interpretation Guide.

L1372: Source: Study Y-47-52120-051
L1373: The SF-36 physical health summary and mental health summary score showed improvement in Dysport subjects at Week 8 when compared to the placebo group and to baseline. The Dysport group reported improvements in all eight individual domains. The largest improvements were in the domains of: role physical (change in mean score 16.6) and bodily pain (change in mean score 13.9). The subjects in the placebo group reported little or no change in all eight individual domains. When summarized into the mental health and physical health summaries, the mean change was small for each section (only ~4 points for the Dysport group in each summary). Since the placebo group physical summary scores were worse than at baseline, the comparison showed a p-value of 0.002 favoring the treatment group. Due to the hierarchical structure used in the analyses, since the mental health summary score (p = 0.061) did not reach statistical significance, the physical health summary could not be considered as statistically significant for the study.
L1374: Proportion of Subjects Considered Treatment Successes
L1375: The proportion of subjects considered treatment success was designated as a secondary endpoint. Overall treatment success was defined by a global efficacy assessment of “better” or “much better”, and a global safety assessment of no worse than “moderate”. The odds ratio represents the odds of success on Dysport versus placebo (stratified for strata and country). The assessment was done using a logistic model with treatment, strata (naïve or non-naïve), and center as factors in the model. The odds ratio of the number of successes on Dysport versus placebo is summarized for the ITT population together with a p-value and 95% CI in Table 20. The investigator’s global assessment of overall treatment success at Week 12 was higher in the Dysport group (32/55 subjects, 58%) than in the placebo group (10/61 subjects, 16%). The odds ratio for overall treatment success rate in the Dysport group compared to placebo was 7.206 (95% CI: 3.009 to 17.260, p &lt; 0.0001). Due to the hierarchical structure used in the analyses, since the score could not be considered as statistically significant for the study.

L1379: Exploratory Endpoints
L1380: Evaluation of the exploratory endpoints
L1381: For the exploratory efficacy endpoints, only a descriptive analysis was to be presented, along with the (unadjusted) 95% CI for the difference between Dysport and placebo.
L1382: Change from Baseline in TWSTRS Subscale Scores (Severity, Disability, Pain)
L1383: The changes from baseline in TWSTRS severity, disability and pain subscale scores at Weeks 4, 8 and 12 were exploratory variables, and are summarized in Table 21 (severity), Table 22 (disability), Table 23 (pain). The mean score improved more for the Dysport group than for the placebo group at Weeks 4 and 8, and to a lesser extent at Week 12 in each of the TWSTRS subscales. .

L1401: Proportion of Responders at Weeks 4, 8, 12
L1402: The proportion of responders was an exploratory endpoint. A responder was defined as a subject with a decrease in TWSTRS-total score of at least 30% compared to baseline. The proportion of responders was greater in the Dysport group than in the placebo group at Weeks 4, 8 and 12, and the difference was statistical significant for Weeks 4 and 8.
L1403: In the Dysport group, 49% were considered responders at both Weeks 4 and 8 (compared to 16% and 11% respectively in the placebo group). Nearly a forth (24%) of the Dysport group were still considered responders at Week 12 (Table 24).

L1407: Change from Baseline in VAS for Pain
L1408: The subject's assessment of pain at Weeks 4, 8 and 12 were exploratory efficacy endpoints. The assessment was made using a VAS of 'no pain (0 mm)' to 'worst possible pain' (100 mm). Both Dysport and placebo group subjects rated their level of pain at about mid-point (47.4 and 49.6 mm respectively) on the 100 mm VAS line at baseline. Table 25 shows that in the Dysport group, the VAS for pain score were decreased at Weeks 4 (-17.7\pm24.4), Week 8 (-15.8\pm30.9), and to a lesser extent, at Week 12 (-7.9\pm27.5). The placebo group reported only a small, consistent change (-4.2 to 4.8 mm) in VAS for pain from baseline to Weeks 4, 8 and 12.

L1415: Subgroup Analyses
L1416: Naïve vs Previously Treated Subjects
L1417: A subgroup analysis was done of subjects who had not been previously treated with botulinum toxin for cervical dystonia, and subjects who had received previous botulinum toxin treatments (naïve and non-naïve subjects, respectively). Within the naïve subpopulation, there were 10 subjects each in the Dysport treatment group and the placebo group. Within the non- naïve subpopulation, there were 45 subjects in the Dysport group and 51 subjects in the placebo group.
L1418: The change from baseline in TWSTRS total score, VAS for symptom assessment, SF-36 mental health QoL and proportion of treatment success at Weeks 4, 8, and 12 of naïve versus non-naïve subjects is summarized in Table 26.
L1419: In Figure 3, the horizontal bars represent the 95% CI of the treatment difference in change from baseline. An improvement with Dysport compared to placebo is indicated if the horizontal line is completely to the right of the zero axis. Dysport demonstrated a treatment benefit compared to placebo for both naïve and non-naïve subgroups. The treatment difference did not reach statistical significance, but that may have been due to the variance associated with small numbers.

L1432: Subjects with Underlying Pain
L1433: A subgroup analysis was conducted to investigate the effect of significant underlying pain on subject response.
L1434: Effect of Underlying Pain on VAS pain rating
L1435: The sponsor explored whether the change in VAS pain scoring would differ for subjects who rated their pain level at &gt;40 mm at baseline. All these subjects had scores lower than baseline at Weeks 4, 8 and 12 (Table 27). The Dysport group had the greater reduction, with the pain rating dipping to nearly half of the baseline rating by Week 8 (baseline = 64.1 (\pm 14.0), Week 8 = 34.8 (\pm 28.2) representing a mean change of -29.7 (\pm 30.0) mm. The placebo group rating inexplicably improved at Week 12, respectively. In this subgroup, the change in pain ratings was greater in the Dysport group showing a greater change for Weeks 4 and 8 than in Week 12, suggesting that efficacy is beginning to decrease between weeks 8 and 12.

L1447: In Dysport subjects with underlying pain, the change from baseline in the TWSTRS total score was greater at Week 4 (-20.2±4.1) than in the corresponding placebo group (-11.6±2.3) or in subjects without underlying pain (-11.6±2.3). The smaller sample size combined with the “placebo effect” for pain treatment may have affected the statistical findings which did not reach levels of statistical significance.
L1448: Time to Retreatment
L1449: Subjects received a single injection of either Dysport or placebo, and time to retreatment was not assessed during this study. Retreatments were measured in follow-on Study Y-47-52120-731, and the efficacy results for that study have not been submitted.
L1450: Changes in the Conduct of the Study or Planned Analyses
L1451: Pregnancy tests occurred at screening as detailed in the schedule of assessments in both the protocol and the RAP, whereas the laboratory section of both documents describe pregnancy tests occurring at baseline.
L1452: 10.1.1.4.5 Safety
L1453: The safety review of all the trials included in the BLA 125274 submission was conducted by Dr. Marc Stone and will be filed separately. The safety review findings for Study 051 were consistent with findings from previous studies done on Botox, Myobloc and Dysport. Dysphagia, neck muscle weakness, and local injection site irritation were the most commonly experienced adverse events. The majority of the adverse events were rated as mild or moderate, occurring within the first 7 to 14 days following injection, and resolved within about 4 weeks.
L1454: 10.1.1.5 Reviewer’s Summary
L1455: Summary and Comments
L1456: Study 051 was well-balanced study for the Dysport and placebo groups in terms of demographics, medical profiles, and previously treated vs drug naïve subjects. The main
L1457: exception (for both primary trials) was the under-representation of non-Caucasian subjects.
L1458: In general, the study design was adequate. There was a limitation in the availability of an early exit allowed after the Week 4 assessments. This was allowed so that subjects not experiencing treatment benefits (possibly due to placebo group inclusion) could be transferred to an open-label extension study. The very high early exit rate was very problematic for the Study 045 Week 8 and Week 12 assessments. Study 051 was conducted (in the US and Russia) after the completion of Study 045. Prior to the commencement of Study 051, the sponsor met with the FDA for discussions on the proposed study. As a result, the early exit rate was lower in Study 051; for the Dysport-treated group (10/55, 18%), and for the placebo group (23/61, 39%). The Week 4 assessment are solid for both of the pivotal trials, but the secondary assessments analyzed from the Study 051 should be considered far more reliable than those of Study 045.
L1459: The primary endpoint of Study 051, the Week 4 change from baseline in the TWSTRS Total score showed a significant difference between the Dysport and the placebo groups (p &lt; 0.0001). The other Week 4 assessments, the subject and investigator VAS for symptoms, also showed greater improvement for the Dysport group than the placebo group at statistically significant levels (both p &lt; 0.001).
L1460: Most of the secondary endpoint analyses (the Week 8 and Week 12 TWSTRS Total scores, subject and investigator VAS scores) also showed improvement in the Dysport group compared to baseline and to placebo group at statistically significant levels. The SF-36 QoL scores were done only at Week 8. The mental health score for QoL favored the Dysport group, but did not reach statistical significance (p = 0.61). It was above the physical health QoL score, and the Week 12 treatment success score in the pre-determined results hierarchy, so the latter two scores were not counted toward the final supportive results, but both showed very significant improvement for the Dysport group.
L1461: By pre-agreement with the sponsor, the exploratory endpoints were not analyzed by p-values, but were characterized by descriptive results and CI intervals. The exploratory endpoints and subgroup analyses included change from baseline in the TWSTRS subscores (severity, disability, and pain), proportion of responders at each visit, comparison of subjects who were treatment-naïve to those previously treated with botulinum toxin, and the effect of underlying pain on study endpoints. The results for the exploratory also showed improvement with Dysport treatment. There were no significant differences in the responses found for previously-treated versus treatment-naïve subjects, or in subjects with underlying pain versus subjects without pain.
L1462: The study defined a “responder” as a subject with at least a 30% decrease in the TWSTRS Total score compared to baseline. By that definition, the percentage of responders was consistently higher at Weeks 4, 8, and 12 in the Dysport group (49%, 49%, and 24% respectively) than in the placebo group (16%, 11%, and 11% respectively).
L1463: Study 051 was sufficiently well-designed and analyzed, and sufficiently consistent in the endpoint results to support the application for Dysport based on clinical efficacy.
L1464: 10.1.2 Study Y-97-52120-045 (Study 45):
L1465: A phase III multicenter, prospective, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study of the efficacy and safety of Dysport (500 units) administered intramuscularly for the treatment of cervical dystonia.
L1466: The study was conducted at 13 centers in the US.
L1467: Sponsor:
L1468: Beaufour-Ipsen R &amp; DIPSEN Limited190 Bath RoadSloughBerkshire, SL1 3XEUKTel: +44 (0) 1753 627 600Fax: +44 (0) 1753 627 859
L1469: Co-ordinating Investigator:
L1470: Drake D. Duane, MDArizona Dystonia Institute10210 North 92nd Street, Suite 300Scottsdale, AZ 85258USATel: +1 480-860-1222Fax: +1 480-860-0029
L1471: Local IRBs were used for each of the centers involved in the study.
L1472: Audit Certificates were provided by (b) (4)
L1473: 10.1.1.1 Objective
L1474: Primary Objective
L1475: To evaluate the efficacy and safety of intramuscular administration of Dysport (500 units) compared to placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia.
L1476: Secondary Objectives
L1477: To evaluate the efficacy of intramuscular administration of DYSPORT (500 units) compared to placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia, as determined by:
L1478: • Change from baseline in TWSTRS-Total score at week 4 (per-protocol population)• Patient visual analogue pain scale at week 4• Patient assessment of change in the signs and symptoms of cervical dystonia at week 4
L1479: Tertiary Objectives
L1480: To evaluate the efficacy of intramuscular administration of DYSPORT (500 units) compared to placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia as determined by:
L1481: • Change from baseline in TWSTRS-Total score at weeks 8 and 12, and
L1482: TWSTRS Sub-scale (severity, disability, pain) scores at weeks 4, 8, and 12
L1483: • Patient visual analogue pain scale at weeks 8 and 12• Proportion of responders at weeks 4, 8, and 12 (Patients were classified as responders if the decrease in TWSTRS-Total score was \geq 30\% and at least 10 points compared with the baseline score.)• Duration of response (time to retreatment eligibility), defined as the time between administration of study medication and the need for retreatment (as determined by a return of the TWSTRS-Total score to within 10% of baseline)• Proportion of patients reporting an improvement in signs and symptoms of cervical dystonia of at least 50% at week 4 compared to baseline• Proportion of patients for whom investigators report an improvement in signs and symptoms of cervical dystonia of at least 50% at week 4 compared to baseline• Patient assessment of change in signs and symptoms of cervical dystonia at weeks 8 and 12• Investigator assessment of change in signs and symptoms of cervical dystonia at weeks 4, 8, and 12• Proportion of patients considered, on completion of the double-blind phase, to be overall treatment successes, as defined by a Global Efficacy Assessment of at least moderate improvement and a Global Safety Assessment of no worse than moderate
L1484: 10.1.1.2 Study Design
L1485: The study was designed as a randomized, double-blind, outpatient, multi-center (16 US sites) trial of a single intramuscular administration of Dysport (500 units) or placebo in adults with cervical dystonia. The study design used two parallel, balanced (~1:1 Dysport to placebo) groups.
L1486: It is especially noteworthy that patients could complete the double blind phase and receive the first open label phase treatment from week 4 onwards.
L1487: The double-blind, placebo-controlled phase was followed by an open-label phase (maximum of three retreatments), and an extended use phase. The objectives of the open-label phase were to determine the safety and efficacy of repeated intramuscular injections of DYSPORT (doses ranging from 250 – 1000 units) for the treatment of cervical dystonia. The double-blind, placebo-controlled phase is the primary focus of the current efficacy review. The safety data analysis includes the first retreatment with DYSPORT in the open-label phase for those patients retreated at week 4 of the double-blind phase (see Safety Review).
L1488: 10.1.1.3 Study Population and Procedures
L1489: The study population was adult (\geq 18 years age) subjects with cervical dystonia including both subjects previously treated with one of the botulinum toxins, and subjects that were treatment naïve. Initially the protocol called for an enrollment of 80 subjects. However, one batch of the study drug (Dysport batch 159J) was below potency requirements, and additional subjects were recruited for the study bringing the total study enrollment to 137 subjects. The subjects were randomized into 3 groups:
L1490: • Group 1 – subjects who did not receive Dysport batch 159J (enrolled after October 16, 1998). Group 1 was the group used for statistical analysis of the primary endpoint.• Group 2 – all subjects regardless of treatment drug received.• Group 3 – subjects receiving treatment on or before October 16, 1998 (possibly with Dysport batch 159J)
L1491: 10.1.1.3.1 Study duration
L1492: Date of first enrolment: April 28, 1998
L1493: Date of last completed: April 14, 2000
L1494: 10.1.1.3.2 Entry criteria
L1495: Inclusion Criteria
L1496: 1. Signed informed consent.2. Male or female of 18 years of age or older.3. Cervical dystonia with at least 18 months since onset, and previously untreated with botulinum toxin or previously treated with BTX-A or botulinum toxin type B (BTX-B) with a minimum interval of 16 weeks since the last injection and having returned at least to their usual pretreatment status.4. TWSTRS scores which met the following criteria at baseline:- TWSTRS total score \geq 30- TWSTRS severity subscale score \geq 15- TWSTRS disability subscale score \geq 3.
L1497: Exclusion Criteria
L1498: 1. Pure antercollis or pure retrocollis.2. Apparent remission from cervical dystonia at time of screening.3. Previous poor response (determined by standard practice at each site, e.g., &lt;20\% improvement in TWSTRS total score from baseline to Week 4) to the previous two BTX-A or BTX-B treatments.4. Known requirement for fewer than 80 or more than 250 units of BOTOX® injected into the neck muscles, or fewer than 4000 or more than 12,500 units of BTX-B.5. Being treated with BTX-B due to lack of efficacy to BTX-A or having known neutralizing antibodies to BTX-A.6. Requirement for botulinum toxin injection to site(s) of the body other than the neck and unable to avoid such treatment(s) for the duration of the study.
L1499: 7. Known hypersensitivity to botulinum toxin or related compounds, or any component in the study drug formulation.
8. Known significant underlying swallowing or respiratory abnormality which might be exacerbated by botulinum toxin treatment.
9. Myasthenia gravis, other disease of the neuromuscular junction or clinically significant, persistent neuromuscular weakness, or disease or symptoms that could interfere with the TWSTRS scoring.
10. Total body weight less than 100 lbs (45.4 kg).
11. Previous phenol injections to the neck muscles.
12. Previous myotomy or denervation surgery involving the neck or shoulder region.
13. Cervical contracture that limits passive range of motion.
14. Treatment with aminoglycoside antibiotics within the 30 days prior to study treatment.
15. Current or expected requirement for concomitant medication that may have interfered with the evaluation of study treatment (e.g., narcotics) (NOTE: Muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines were permitted if the dosage had been stable for 6 weeks prior to study treatment and was expected to remain at this stable dose until the Week 4 assessment. Every effort was to be made to keep concomitant cervical dystonia treatment constant throughout the study; however, changes in pain medication were acceptable if absolutely necessary according to clinical judgment).
16. Previously received any investigational new drug or device within 30 days prior to inclusion in the study.
17. Previously treated in this study.
18. Females who were pregnant or lactating. Women of child-bearing potential must have had a negative prestudy urine pregnancy test and subjects, or their partner, must have agreed to use adequate contraception (hormonal or barrier method of birth control) prior to injection of the study drug and for the duration of study participation. Non-child-bearing potential was defined as post-menopause for at least 1 year, surgical sterilization at least 3 months prior to screening, or hysterectomy.
19. Any medical condition or laboratory finding that compromised compliance with the objectives and procedures of this protocol or precluded the administration of botulinum toxin, as judged by the investigator.
20. In the opinion of the investigator, subjects who were unable and/or unwilling to comply fully with the study protocol (Section 16.1.1) and the study instructions.
L1500: 10.1.1.3.3 Study medications
L1501: Test product, dose and mode of administration, batch number
L1502: • Dysport 500 U
• Placebo
L1503: DYSPORT batches 159J, 179, and 195 were used in the double-blind phase at 500 units mixed with sterile saline for intramuscular injection, divided among 3 to 4 CD affected cervical muscles. In the open-label phase the initial DYSPORT dose was 500 units.
L1504: DYSPORT batches 159J, 179, and 195 were used for the first dose in the open-label phase. The initial DYSPORT batch (159J) fell below the minimum potency specification during the enrollment of the first 57 patients in the study. At the recommendation of the FDA, the sponsor added 80 patients must be enrolled to ensure a the adequate number of subjects receiving Dysport of known potency for statistical analysis.
L1505: The placebo was provided to match the study drug product in properties such as color, reconstitution and injection requirements.
L1506: Prohibited concurrent therapy
L1507: Subjects were not allowed to receive:
L1508: • botulinum toxin to any site of the body other than the neck,
• any investigational new drug or device,
• treatment with aminoglycoside antibiotics,
• any other drug that interferes with neuromuscular transmission
L1509: Permitted concomitant therapy
L1510: Subjects were permitted to receive:
L1511: • Muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines, if the dose had been stable for 6 weeks before study entry and was expected to remain stable.
• A short course of muscle relaxants and benzodiazepines if required for patient care.
